11. 
12. using SDKTemplate;
13. using System;
14. using System.Collections.Generic;
15. using System.Threading.Tasks;
16. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
17. using Windows.Devices.Midi;
18. using Windows.UI.Core;
19. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
20. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
21. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
22. 
23. namespace MIDI
24. {
25.     /// <summary>
26.     /// DeviceWatcher class to monitor adding/removing MIDI devices on the fly
27.     /// </summary>
28.     internal class MidiDeviceWatcher
29.     {
30.         internal DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
31.         internal DeviceInformationCollection deviceInformationCollection = null;
32.         bool enumerationCompleted = false;
33.         ListBox portList = null;
34.         string midiSelector = string.Empty;
35.         // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
36.         CoreDispatcher coreDispatcher = null;
37. 
38.         /// <summary>
39.         /// Constructor: Initialize and hook up Device Watcher events
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="midiSelectorString">MIDI Device Selector</param>
42.         /// <param name="dispatcher">CoreDispatcher instance, to update UI thread</param>
43.         /// <param name="portListBox">The UI element to update with list of devices</param>
44.         // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
45.         internal MidiDeviceWatcher(string midiSelectorString, CoreDispatcher dispatcher, ListBox portListBox)
46.         {
47.             this.deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(midiSelectorString);
48.             this.portList = portListBox;
49.             this.midiSelector = midiSelectorString;
50.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
51.             this.coreDispatcher = dispatcher;
52. 
53.             this.deviceWatcher.Added += DeviceWatcher_Added;
54.             this.deviceWatcher.Removed += DeviceWatcher_Removed;
55.             this.deviceWatcher.Updated += DeviceWatcher_Updated;
56.             this.deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted += DeviceWatcher_EnumerationCompleted;
57.         }
58. 
59.         /// <summary>
60.         /// Destructor: Remove Device Watcher events
61.         /// </summary>
62.         ~MidiDeviceWatcher()
63.         {
64.             this.deviceWatcher.Added -= DeviceWatcher_Added;
65.             this.deviceWatcher.Removed -= DeviceWatcher_Removed;
66.             this.deviceWatcher.Updated -= DeviceWatcher_Updated;
67.             this.deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted -= DeviceWatcher_EnumerationCompleted;
68.         }
69. 
70.         /// <summary>
71.         /// Start the Device Watcher
72.         /// </summary>
73.         internal void Start()
74.         {
75.             if(this.deviceWatcher.Status != DeviceWatcherStatus.Started)
76.             {
77.                 this.deviceWatcher.Start();
78.             }
79.         }
80. 
81.         /// <summary>
82.         /// Stop the Device Watcher
83.         /// </summary>
84.         internal void Stop()
85.         {
86.             if(this.deviceWatcher.Status != DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopped)
87.             {
88.                 this.deviceWatcher.Stop();
89.             }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Get the DeviceInformationCollection
94.         /// </summary>
95.         /// <returns></returns>
96.         internal DeviceInformationCollection GetDeviceInformationCollection()
97.         {
98.             return this.deviceInformationCollection;
99.         }
100. 
101.         /// <summary>
102.         /// Add any connected MIDI devices to the list
103.         /// </summary>
104.         private async void UpdateDevices()
105.         {
106.             // Get a list of all MIDI devices
107.             this.deviceInformationCollection = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(this.midiSelector);
108. 
109.             // If no devices are found, update the ListBox
110.             if ((this.deviceInformationCollection == null) || (this.deviceInformationCollection.Count == 0))
111.             {
112.                 // Start with a clean list
113.                 this.portList.Items.Clear();
114. 
115.                 this.portList.Items.Add("No MIDI ports found");
116.                 this.portList.IsEnabled = false;
117.             }
118.             // If devices are found, enumerate them and add them to the list
119.             else
120.             {
121.                 // Start with a clean list
122.                 this.portList.Items.Clear();
123. 
124.                 foreach (var device in deviceInformationCollection)
125.                 {
126.                     this.portList.Items.Add(device.Name);
127.                 }
128. 
129.                 this.portList.IsEnabled = true;
130.             }
131.         }
132. 
133.         /// <summary>
134.         /// Update UI on device added
135.         /// </summary>
136.         /// <param name="sender">The active DeviceWatcher instance</param>
137.         /// <param name="args">Event arguments</param>
138.         private async void DeviceWatcher_Added(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformation args)
139.         {
140.             // If all devices have been enumerated
141.             if (this.enumerationCompleted)
142.             {
143.                 // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
144.                 await coreDispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.High, () =>
145.                 {
146.                     // Update the device list
147.                     UpdateDevices();
148.                 });
149.             }
150.         }
151. 
152.         /// <summary>
153.         /// Update UI on device removed
154.         /// </summary>
155.         /// <param name="sender">The active DeviceWatcher instance</param>
156.         /// <param name="args">Event arguments</param>
157.         private async void DeviceWatcher_Removed(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate args)
158.         {
159.             // If all devices have been enumerated
160.             if (this.enumerationCompleted)
161.             {
162.                 // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
163.                 await coreDispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.High, () =>
164.                 {
165.                     // Update the device list
166.                     UpdateDevices();
167.                 });
168.             }
169.         }
170. 
171.         /// <summary>
172.         /// Update UI on device updated
173.         /// </summary>
174.         /// <param name="sender">The active DeviceWatcher instance</param>
175.         /// <param name="args">Event arguments</param>
176.         private async void DeviceWatcher_Updated(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate args)
177.         {
178.             // If all devices have been enumerated
179.             if (this.enumerationCompleted)
180.             {
181.                 // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
182.                 await coreDispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.High, () =>
183.                 {
184.                     // Update the device list
185.                     UpdateDevices();
186.                 });
187.             }
188.         }
189. 
190.         /// <summary>
191.         /// Update UI on device enumeration completed.
192.         /// </summary>
193.         /// <param name="sender">The active DeviceWatcher instance</param>
194.         /// <param name="args">Event arguments</param>
195.         private async void DeviceWatcher_EnumerationCompleted(DeviceWatcher sender, object args)
196.         {
197.             this.enumerationCompleted = true;
198.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
199.             await coreDispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.High, () =>
200.             {
201.                 // Update the device list

12. using SDKTemplate;
13. using System;
14. using System.Collections.Generic;
15. using System.Text;
16. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
17. using Windows.Devices.Midi;
18. using Windows.Storage.Streams;
19. using Windows.UI.Core;
20. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
21. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
22. 
23. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=234238
24. 
25. namespace MIDI
26. {
27.     /// <summary>
28.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
29.     /// </summary>
30.     public sealed partial class Scenario2_ReceiveMIDIMessages : Page
31.     {
32.         /// <summary>
33.         /// Main Page 
34.         /// </summary>
35.         private MainPage rootPage;
36. 
37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Collection of active MidiInPorts
39.         /// </summary>
40.         private List<MidiInPort> midiInPorts;
41. 
42.         /// <summary>
43.         /// Device watcher for MIDI in ports
44.         /// </summary>
45.         MidiDeviceWatcher midiInDeviceWatcher;
46. 
47.         /// <summary>
48.         /// Set the rootpage context when entering the scenario
49.         /// </summary>
50.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
51.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
52.         {
53.             this.rootPage = MainPage.Current;
54.         }
55. 
56.         /// <summary>
57.         /// Stop the device watcher when leaving the scenario
58.         /// </summary>
59.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
60.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
61.         {
62.             base.OnNavigatedFrom(e);
63. 
64.             // Stop the input device watcher
65.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Stop();
66. 
67.             // Close all MidiInPorts
68.             foreach (MidiInPort inPort in this.midiInPorts)
69.             {
70.                 inPort.Dispose();
71.             }
72.             this.midiInPorts.Clear();
73.         }
74. 
75.         /// <summary>
76.         /// Constructor: Start the device watcher
77.         /// </summary>
78.         public Scenario2_ReceiveMIDIMessages()
79.         {
80.             this.InitializeComponent();
81. 
82.             // Initialize the list of active MIDI input devices
83.             this.midiInPorts = new List<MidiInPort>();
84. 
85.             // Set up the MIDI input device watcher
86.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher = new MidiDeviceWatcher(MidiInPort.GetDeviceSelector(), Dispatcher, this.inputDevices);
87. 
88.             // Start watching for devices
89.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Start();
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Change the input MIDI device from which to receive messages
94.         /// </summary>
95.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
96.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
97.         private async void inputDevices_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
98.         {
99.             // Get the selected input MIDI device
100.             int selectedInputDeviceIndex = (sender as ListBox).SelectedIndex;
101. 
102.             // Try to create a MidiInPort
103.             if (selectedInputDeviceIndex < 0)
104.             {
105.                 // Clear input device messages
106.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Clear();
107.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Add("Select a MIDI input device to be able to see its messages");
108.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.IsEnabled = false;
109.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Select a MIDI input device to be able to see its messages", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
110.                 return;
111.             }
112. 
113.             DeviceInformationCollection devInfoCollection = this.midiInDeviceWatcher.GetDeviceInformationCollection();
114.             if (devInfoCollection == null)
115.             {
116.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Clear();
117.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Add("Device not found!");
118.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.IsEnabled = false;
119.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
120.                 return;
121.             }
122. 
123.             DeviceInformation devInfo = devInfoCollection[selectedInputDeviceIndex];
124.             if (devInfo == null)
125.             {
126.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Clear();
127.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Add("Device not found!");
128.                 this.inputDeviceMessages.IsEnabled = false;
129.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
130.                 return;
131.             }
132. 
133.             var currentMidiInputDevice = await MidiInPort.FromIdAsync(devInfo.Id);
134.             if (currentMidiInputDevice == null)
135.             {
136.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Unable to create MidiInPort from input device", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
137.                 return;
138.             }
139. 
140.             // We have successfully created a MidiInPort; add the device to the list of active devices, and set up message receiving
141.             if (!this.midiInPorts.Contains(currentMidiInputDevice))
142.             {
143.                 this.midiInPorts.Add(currentMidiInputDevice);
144.                 currentMidiInputDevice.MessageReceived += MidiInputDevice_MessageReceived;
145.             }
146. 
147.             // Clear any previous input messages
148.             this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Clear();
149.             this.inputDeviceMessages.IsEnabled = true;
150. 
151.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Input Device selected successfully! Waiting for messages...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
152.         }
153. 
154.         /// <summary>
155.         /// Display the received MIDI message in a readable format
156.         /// </summary>
157.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
158.         /// <param name="args">The received message</param>
159.         private async void MidiInputDevice_MessageReceived(MidiInPort sender, MidiMessageReceivedEventArgs args)
160.         {
161.             IMidiMessage receivedMidiMessage = args.Message;
162. 
163.             // Build the received MIDI message into a readable format
164.             StringBuilder outputMessage = new StringBuilder();
165.             outputMessage.Append(receivedMidiMessage.Timestamp.ToString()).Append(", Type: ").Append(receivedMidiMessage.Type);
166. 
167.             // Add MIDI message parameters to the output, depending on the type of message
168.             switch (receivedMidiMessage.Type)
169.             {
170.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOff:
171.                     var noteOffMessage = (MidiNoteOffMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
172.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(noteOffMessage.Channel).Append(", Note: ").Append(noteOffMessage.Note).Append(", Velocity: ").Append(noteOffMessage.Velocity);
173.                     break;
174.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOn:
175.                     var noteOnMessage = (MidiNoteOnMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
176.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(noteOnMessage.Channel).Append(", Note: ").Append(noteOnMessage.Note).Append(", Velocity: ").Append(noteOnMessage.Velocity);
177.                     break;
178.                 case MidiMessageType.PolyphonicKeyPressure:
179.                     var polyphonicKeyPressureMessage = (MidiPolyphonicKeyPressureMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
180.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(polyphonicKeyPressureMessage.Channel).Append(", Note: ").Append(polyphonicKeyPressureMessage.Note).Append(", Pressure: ").Append(polyphonicKeyPressureMessage.Pressure);
181.                     break;
182.                 case MidiMessageType.ControlChange:
183.                     var controlChangeMessage = (MidiControlChangeMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
184.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(controlChangeMessage.Channel).Append(", Controller: ").Append(controlChangeMessage.Controller).Append(", Value: ").Append(controlChangeMessage.ControlValue);
185.                     break;
186.                 case MidiMessageType.ProgramChange:
187.                     var programChangeMessage = (MidiProgramChangeMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
188.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(programChangeMessage.Channel).Append(", Program: ").Append(programChangeMessage.Program);
189.                     break;
190.                 case MidiMessageType.ChannelPressure:
191.                     var channelPressureMessage = (MidiChannelPressureMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
192.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(channelPressureMessage.Channel).Append(", Pressure: ").Append(channelPressureMessage.Pressure);
193.                     break;
194.                 case MidiMessageType.PitchBendChange:
195.                     var pitchBendChangeMessage = (MidiPitchBendChangeMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
196.                     outputMessage.Append(", Channel: ").Append(pitchBendChangeMessage.Channel).Append(", Bend: ").Append(pitchBendChangeMessage.Bend);
197.                     break;
198.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemExclusive:
199.                     var systemExclusiveMessage = (MidiSystemExclusiveMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
200.                     outputMessage.Append(", ");
201. 
202.                     // Read the SysEx bufffer
203.                     var sysExDataReader = DataReader.FromBuffer(systemExclusiveMessage.RawData);
204.                     while (sysExDataReader.UnconsumedBufferLength > 0)
205.                     {
206.                         byte byteRead = sysExDataReader.ReadByte();
207.                         // Pad with leading zero if necessary
208.                         outputMessage.Append(byteRead.ToString("X2")).Append(" ");
209.                     }
210.                     break;
211.                 case MidiMessageType.MidiTimeCode:
212.                     var timeCodeMessage = (MidiTimeCodeMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
213.                     outputMessage.Append(", FrameType: ").Append(timeCodeMessage.FrameType).Append(", Values: ").Append(timeCodeMessage.Values);
214.                     break;
215.                 case MidiMessageType.SongPositionPointer:
216.                     var songPositionPointerMessage = (MidiSongPositionPointerMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
217.                     outputMessage.Append(", Beats: ").Append(songPositionPointerMessage.Beats);
218.                     break;
219.                 case MidiMessageType.SongSelect:
220.                     var songSelectMessage = (MidiSongSelectMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
221.                     outputMessage.Append(", Song: ").Append(songSelectMessage.Song);
222.                     break;
223.                 case MidiMessageType.TuneRequest:
224.                     var tuneRequestMessage = (MidiTuneRequestMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
225.                     break;
226.                 case MidiMessageType.TimingClock:
227.                     var timingClockMessage = (MidiTimingClockMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
228.                     break;
229.                 case MidiMessageType.Start:
230.                     var startMessage = (MidiStartMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
231.                     break;
232.                 case MidiMessageType.Continue:
233.                     var continueMessage = (MidiContinueMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
234.                     break;
235.                 case MidiMessageType.Stop:
236.                     var stopMessage = (MidiStopMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
237.                     break;
238.                 case MidiMessageType.ActiveSensing:
239.                     var activeSensingMessage = (MidiActiveSensingMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
240.                     break;
241.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemReset:
242.                     var systemResetMessage = (MidiSystemResetMessage)receivedMidiMessage;
243.                     break;
244.                 case MidiMessageType.None:
245.                     throw new InvalidOperationException();
246.                 default:
247.                     break;
248.             }
249. 
250.             // Use the Dispatcher to update the messages on the UI thread
251.             // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
252.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.High, () =>
253.             {
254.                 // Skip TimingClock and ActiveSensing messages to avoid overcrowding the list. Commment this check out to see all messages
255.                 if ((receivedMidiMessage.Type != MidiMessageType.TimingClock) && (receivedMidiMessage.Type != MidiMessageType.ActiveSensing))
256.                 {
257.                     this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Add(outputMessage + "\n");
258.                     this.inputDeviceMessages.ScrollIntoView(this.inputDeviceMessages.Items[this.inputDeviceMessages.Items.Count - 1]);
259.                     this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Message received successfully!", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
260.                 }
261.             });
262.         }
263.     }
264. }
265.

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

36.         /// Device watchers for MIDI in and out ports
37.         /// </summary>
38.         MidiDeviceWatcher midiInDeviceWatcher;
39.         MidiDeviceWatcher midiOutDeviceWatcher;
40. 
41.         /// <summary>
42.         /// Set the rootpage context when entering the scenario
43.         /// </summary>
44.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
45.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
46.         {
47.             this.rootPage = MainPage.Current;
48.         }
49. 
50.         /// <summary>
51.         /// Stop the device watchers when leaving the scenario
52.         /// </summary>
53.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
54.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
55.         {
56.             base.OnNavigatedFrom(e);
57. 
58.             // Stop the input and output device watchers
59.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Stop();
60.             this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.Stop();
61.         }
62. 
63.         /// <summary>
64.         /// Constructor: Empty device lists, start the device watchers and
65.         /// set initial states for buttons
66.         /// </summary>
67.         public Scenario1_MIDIDeviceEnumeration()
68.         {
69.             this.InitializeComponent();
70. 
71.             // Start with a clean slate
72.             ClearAllDeviceValues();
73. 
74.             // Ensure Auto-detect devices toggle is on
75.             this.deviceAutoDetectToggle.IsOn = true;
76. 
77.             // Set up the MIDI input and output device watchers
78.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher = new MidiDeviceWatcher(MidiInPort.GetDeviceSelector(), Dispatcher, this.inputDevices);
79.             this.midiOutDeviceWatcher = new MidiDeviceWatcher(MidiOutPort.GetDeviceSelector(), Dispatcher, this.outputDevices);
80. 
81.             // Start watching for devices
82.             this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Start();
83.             this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.Start();
84. 
85.             // Disable manual enumeration buttons
86.             this.listInputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = false;
87.             this.listOutputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = false;
88.         }
89. 
90.         /// <summary>
91.         /// Clear all input and output MIDI device lists and properties
92.         /// </summary>
93.         private void ClearAllDeviceValues()
94.         {
95.             // Clear input devices
96.             this.inputDevices.Items.Clear();
97.             this.inputDevices.Items.Add("Click button to list input MIDI devices");
98.             this.inputDevices.IsEnabled = false;
99. 
100.             // Clear output devices
101.             this.outputDevices.Items.Clear();
102.             this.outputDevices.Items.Add("Click button to list output MIDI devices");
103.             this.outputDevices.IsEnabled = false;
104. 
105.             // Clear input device properties
106.             this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
107.             this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Select a MIDI input device to view its properties");
108.             this.inputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
109. 
110.             // Clear output device properties
111.             this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
112.             this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Select a MIDI output device to view its properties");
113.             this.outputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
114.         }
115. 
116.         /// <summary>
117.         /// Input button click handler
118.         /// </summary>
119.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
120.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
121.         private async void listInputDevicesButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
122.         {
123.             // Enumerate input devices
124.             await EnumerateMidiInputDevices();
125.         }
126. 
127.         /// <summary>
128.         /// Query DeviceInformation class for Midi Input devices
129.         /// </summary>
130.         private async Task EnumerateMidiInputDevices()
131.         {
132.             // Clear input devices
133.             this.inputDevices.Items.Clear();
134.             this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
135.             this.inputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
136. 
137.             // Find all input MIDI devices
138.             string midiInputQueryString = MidiInPort.GetDeviceSelector();
139.             DeviceInformationCollection midiInputDevices = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(midiInputQueryString);
140. 
141.             // Return if no external devices are connected
142.             if (midiInputDevices.Count == 0)
143.             {
144.                 this.inputDevices.Items.Add("No MIDI input devices found!");
145.                 this.inputDevices.IsEnabled = false;
146. 
147.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Please connect at least one external MIDI device for this demo to work correctly", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
148.                 return;
149.             }
150. 
151.             // Else, add each connected input device to the list
152.             foreach (DeviceInformation deviceInfo in midiInputDevices)
153.             {
154.                 this.inputDevices.Items.Add(deviceInfo.Name);
155.                 this.inputDevices.IsEnabled = true;
156.             }
157. 
158.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("MIDI Input devices found!", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
159.         }
160. 
161.         /// <summary>
162.         /// Output button click handler
163.         /// </summary>
164.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
165.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
166.         private async void listOutputDevicesButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
167.         {
168.             // Enumerate output devices
169.             await EnumerateMidiOutputDevices();
170.         }
171. 
172.         /// <summary>
173.         /// Query DeviceInformation class for Midi Output devices
174.         /// </summary>
175.         private async Task EnumerateMidiOutputDevices()
176.         {
177.             // Clear output devices
178.             this.outputDevices.Items.Clear();
179.             this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
180.             this.outputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
181. 
182.             // Find all output MIDI devices
183.             string midiOutputQueryString = MidiOutPort.GetDeviceSelector();
184.             DeviceInformationCollection midiOutputDevices = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(midiOutputQueryString);
185. 
186.             // Return if no external devices are connected, and GS synth is not detected
187.             if (midiOutputDevices.Count == 0)
188.             {
189.                 this.outputDevices.Items.Add("No MIDI output devices found!");
190.                 this.outputDevices.IsEnabled = false;
191. 
192.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Please connect at least one external MIDI device for this demo to work correctly", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
193.                 return;
194.             }
195. 
196.             // List specific device information for each output device
197.             foreach (DeviceInformation deviceInfo in midiOutputDevices)
198.             {
199.                 this.outputDevices.Items.Add(deviceInfo.Name);
200.                 this.outputDevices.IsEnabled = true;
201.             }
202. 
203.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("MIDI Output devices found!", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
204.         }
205. 
206.         /// <summary>
207.         /// Detect the toggle state of the Devicewatcher button.
208.         /// If auto-detect is on, disable manual enumeration buttons and start device watchers
209.         /// If auto-detect is off, enable manual enumeration buttons and stop device watchers
210.         /// </summary>
211.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
212.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
213.         private void DeviceAutoDetectToggle_Toggled(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
214.         {
215.             if (this.deviceAutoDetectToggle.IsOn)
216.             {
217.                 this.listInputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = false;
218.                 this.listOutputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = false;
219. 
220.                 if (this.midiInDeviceWatcher != null)
221.                 {
222.                     this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Start();
223.                 }
224.                 if (this.midiOutDeviceWatcher != null)
225.                 {
226.                     this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.Start();
227.                 }
228.             }
229.             else
230.             {
231.                 this.listInputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = true;
232.                 this.listOutputDevicesButton.IsEnabled = true;
233. 
234.                 if (this.midiInDeviceWatcher != null)
235.                 {
236.                     this.midiInDeviceWatcher.Stop();
237.                 }
238.                 if (this.midiOutDeviceWatcher != null)
239.                 {
240.                     this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.Stop();
241.                 }
242.             }
243.         }
244. 
245.         /// <summary>
246.         /// Change the active input MIDI device
247.         /// </summary>
248.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
249.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
250.         private void inputDevices_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
251.         {
252.             // Get the selected input MIDI device
253.             int selectedInputDeviceIndex = this.inputDevices.SelectedIndex;
254. 
255.             // Try to display the appropriate device properties
256.             if (selectedInputDeviceIndex < 0)
257.             {
258.                 // Clear input device properties
259.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
260.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Select a MIDI input device to view its properties");
261.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
262.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Select a MIDI input device to view its properties", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
263.                 return;
264.             }
265. 
266.             DeviceInformationCollection devInfoCollection = this.midiInDeviceWatcher.GetDeviceInformationCollection();
267.             if (devInfoCollection == null)
268.             {
269.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
270.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Device not found!");
271.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
272.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
273.                 return;
274.             }
275. 
276.             DeviceInformation devInfo = devInfoCollection[selectedInputDeviceIndex];
277.             if (devInfo == null)
278.             {
279.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
280.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Device not found!");
281.                 this.inputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
282.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
283.                 return;
284.             }
285. 
286.             // Display the found properties
287.             DisplayDeviceProperties(devInfo, this.inputDeviceProperties);
288.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device information found!", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
289.         }
290. 
291.         /// <summary>
292.         /// Change the active output MIDI device
293.         /// </summary>
294.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
295.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
296.         private void outputDevices_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
297.         {
298.             // Get the selected output MIDI device
299.             int selectedOutputDeviceIndex = this.outputDevices.SelectedIndex;
300. 
301.             // Try to display the appropriate device properties
302.             if (selectedOutputDeviceIndex < 0)
303.             {
304.                 // Clear output device properties
305.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
306.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Select a MIDI output device to view its properties");
307.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
308.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Select a MIDI output device to view its properties", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
309.                 return;
310.             }
311. 
312.             DeviceInformationCollection devInfoCollection = this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.GetDeviceInformationCollection();
313.             if (devInfoCollection == null)
314.             {
315.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
316.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Device not found!");
317.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
318.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
319.                 return;
320.             }
321. 
322.             DeviceInformation devInfo = devInfoCollection[selectedOutputDeviceIndex];
323.             if (devInfo == null)
324.             {
325.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Clear();
326.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.Items.Add("Device not found!");
327.                 this.outputDeviceProperties.IsEnabled = false;
328.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
329.                 return;
330.             }
331. 
332.             // Display the found properties
333.             DisplayDeviceProperties(devInfo, this.outputDeviceProperties);
334.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device information found!", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
335.         }
336. 
337.         /// <summary>
338.         /// Display the properties of the MIDI device to the user
339.         /// </summary>
340.         /// <param name="devInfo"></param>
341.         /// <param name="propertiesList"></param>
342.         private void DisplayDeviceProperties(DeviceInformation devInfo, ListBox propertiesList)
343.         {
344.             propertiesList.Items.Clear();
345.             propertiesList.Items.Add("Id: " + devInfo.Id);
346.             propertiesList.Items.Add("Name: " + devInfo.Name);
347.             propertiesList.Items.Add("IsDefault: " + devInfo.IsDefault);
348.             propertiesList.Items.Add("IsEnabled: " + devInfo.IsEnabled);
349.             propertiesList.Items.Add("EnclosureLocation: " + devInfo.EnclosureLocation);
350. 
351.             // Add device interface information
352.             propertiesList.Items.Add("----Device Interface----");
353.             foreach (var deviceProperty in devInfo.Properties)
354.             {
355.                 propertiesList.Items.Add(deviceProperty.Key + ": " + deviceProperty.Value);
356.             }
357. 
358.             propertiesList.IsEnabled = true;
359.         }
360.     }
361. }

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates

- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.

### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:

- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.

Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.

### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually have

The WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).

If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires runFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capability>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matter what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern).

5. **Carry over every UWP `<Extension>` the app's behavior depends on.** The scaffold manifest has none; the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` are activation/registration prerequisites — omitting them makes the feature fail **at runtime** (often as a silently dead control), not at build time. Copy each `<Extension>` block from the UWP source manifest (under `.uwp-source/` or the copied UWP `Package.appxmanifest`) into the WinUI 3 root manifest's `<Application>`. Common ones:

   | Extension `Category` | Feature it powers |
   |----------------------|-------------------|
   | `windows.backgroundTasks` | out-of-process `BackgroundTaskBuilder` tasks (one `<Extension EntryPoint="...">` per `TaskEntryPoint`) |
   | `windows.protocol` | custom URI-scheme activation |
   | `windows.fileTypeAssociation` | open-with / file activation |
   | `windows.appService` | background app services |
   | `windows.shareTarget` | receiving shared content |

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` FAILs if retained `BackgroundTaskBuilder` code has no `windows.backgroundTasks` extension; the other categories have no automated check, so verify them by diffing the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` against the migrated one.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Threading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Search for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires ALT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalent requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code constants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Migrating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites just the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHelper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavigationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, the LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using Windows.UI.Core;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;
17. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
18. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Data;
19. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
20. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
21. 
22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }

92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Constructor: Start the device watcher and populate MIDI message types
94.         /// </summary>
95.         public Scenario3_SendMIDIMessages()
96.         {
97.             this.InitializeComponent();
98. 
99.             // Initialize the list of active MIDI output devices
100.             this.midiOutPorts = new List<IMidiOutPort>();
101. 
102.             // Set up the MIDI output device watcher

112.         /// <summary>

114.         /// and populate the MIDI message combo box
115.         /// </summary>
116.         private void PopulateMessageTypes()
117.         {
118.             // Build the list of available MIDI messages for reverse lookup later
119.             this.messageTypes = new Dictionary<MidiMessageType, string>();
120.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.ActiveSensing, "Active Sensing");
121.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.ChannelPressure, "Channel Pressure");
122.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.Continue, "Continue");
123.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.ControlChange, "Control Change");
124.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.MidiTimeCode, "MIDI Time Code");
125.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.NoteOff, "Note Off");
126.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.NoteOn, "Note On");
127.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.PitchBendChange, "Pitch Bend Change");
128.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.PolyphonicKeyPressure, "Polyphonic Key Pressure");
129.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.ProgramChange, "Program Change");
130.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.SongPositionPointer, "Song Position Pointer");
131.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.SongSelect, "Song Select");
132.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.Start, "Start");
133.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.Stop, "Stop");
134.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.SystemExclusive, "System Exclusive");
135.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.SystemReset, "System Reset");
136.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.TimingClock, "Timing Clock");
137.             this.messageTypes.Add(MidiMessageType.TuneRequest, "Tune Request");
138. 
139.             // Start with a clean slate
140.             this.messageType.Items.Clear();
141. 
142.             // Add the message types to the list
143.             foreach (var messageType in this.messageTypes)
144.             {
145.                 this.messageType.Items.Add(messageType.Value);
146.             }
147.         }

149.         /// <summary>
150.         /// Create a new MidiOutPort for the selected device
151.         /// </summary>
152.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
153.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
154.         private async void outputDevices_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
155.         {
156.             // Get the selected output MIDI device
157.             int selectedOutputDeviceIndex = this.outputDevices.SelectedIndex;
158. 
159.             // Try to create a MidiOutPort
160.             if (selectedOutputDeviceIndex < 0)
161.             {
162.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Select a MIDI output device to be able to send messages to it", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
163.                 return;
164.             }
165. 
166.             DeviceInformationCollection devInfoCollection = this.midiOutDeviceWatcher.GetDeviceInformationCollection();
167.             if (devInfoCollection == null)
168.             {
169.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
170.                 return;
171.             }
172. 
173.             DeviceInformation devInfo = devInfoCollection[selectedOutputDeviceIndex];
174.             if (devInfo == null)
175.             {
176.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Device not found!", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
177.                 return;
178.             }
179. 
180.             this.currentMidiOutputDevice = await MidiOutPort.FromIdAsync(devInfo.Id);
181.             if (this.currentMidiOutputDevice == null)
182.             {
183.                 this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Unable to create MidiOutPort from output device", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
184.                 return;
185.             }
186.             
187.             // We have successfully created a MidiOutPort; add the device to the list of active devices
188.             if (!this.midiOutPorts.Contains(this.currentMidiOutputDevice))
189.             {
190.                 this.midiOutPorts.Add(this.currentMidiOutputDevice);
191.             }
192. 
193.             // Enable message type list & reset button
194.             this.messageType.IsEnabled = true;
195.             this.resetButton.IsEnabled = true;
196. 
197.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Output Device selected successfully! Waiting for message type selection...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
198.         }
199.

276.                     break;
277.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemExclusive:
278.                     var dataWriter = new DataWriter();
279.                     var sysExMessage = this.sysExMessageContent.Text;
280.                     var sysExMessageLength = sysExMessage.Length;
281. 
282.                     // Do not send a blank SysEx message
283.                     if (sysExMessageLength == 0)
284.                     {
285.                         return;
286.                     }
287. 
288.                     // SysEx messages are two characters long with 1-character space in between them
289.                     // So we add 1 to the message length, so that it is perfectly divisible by 3
290.                     // The loop count tracks the number of individual message pieces
291.                     int loopCount = (sysExMessageLength + 1) / 3;
292. 
293.                     // Expecting a string of format "F0 NN NN NN NN.... F7", where NN is a byte in hex
294.                     for (int i = 0; i < loopCount; i++)
295.                     {
296.                         var messageString = sysExMessage.Substring(3 * i, 2);
297.                         var messageByte = Convert.ToByte(messageString, 16);
298.                         dataWriter.WriteByte(messageByte);
299.                     }
300.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiSystemExclusiveMessage(dataWriter.DetachBuffer());
301.                     break;
302.                 case MidiMessageType.MidiTimeCode:
303.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiTimeCodeMessage(Convert.ToByte(this.parameter1.SelectedValue), Convert.ToByte(this.parameter2.SelectedValue));
304.                     break;
305.                 case MidiMessageType.SongPositionPointer:
306.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiSongPositionPointerMessage(Convert.ToUInt16(this.parameter1.SelectedValue));
307.                     break;
308.                 case MidiMessageType.SongSelect:
309.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiSongSelectMessage(Convert.ToByte(this.parameter1.SelectedValue));
310.                     break;
311.                 case MidiMessageType.TuneRequest:
312.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiTuneRequestMessage();
313.                     break;
314.                 case MidiMessageType.TimingClock:
315.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiTimingClockMessage();
316.                     break;
317.                 case MidiMessageType.Start:
318.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiStartMessage();
319.                     break;
320.                 case MidiMessageType.Continue:
321.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiContinueMessage();
322.                     break;
323.                 case MidiMessageType.Stop:
324.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiStopMessage();
325.                     break;
326.                 case MidiMessageType.ActiveSensing:
327.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiActiveSensingMessage();
328.                     break;
329.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemReset:
330.                     midiMessageToSend = new MidiSystemResetMessage();
331.                     break;
332.                 case MidiMessageType.None:
333.                 default:
334.                     return;
335.             }
336.

338.             this.currentMidiOutputDevice.SendMessage(midiMessageToSend);
339.             this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Message sent successfully", NotifyType.StatusMessage);

342.         /// <summary>

344.         /// depending on the type of message
345.         /// </summary>
346.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
347.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
348.         private void messageType_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
349.         {
350.             // Find the index of the user's choice
351.             int messageTypeSelectedIndex = this.messageType.SelectedIndex;
352. 
353.             // Return if reset
354.             if (messageTypeSelectedIndex == -1)
355.             {
356.                 return;
357.             }
358. 
359.             // Clear the UI
360.             ResetMessageTypeAndParameters(false);
361. 
362.             // Find the key by index; that's our message type
363.             int count = 0;
364.             foreach (var messageType in messageTypes)
365.             {
366.                 if (messageTypeSelectedIndex == count)
367.                 {
368.                     this.currentMessageType = messageType.Key;
369.                     break;
370.                 }
371.                 count++;
372.             }
373. 
374.             // Some MIDI message types don't need additional parameters
375.             // For them, show the Send button as soon as user selects message type from the list
376.             switch (this.currentMessageType)
377.             {
378.                 // SysEx messages need to be in a particular format
379.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemExclusive:
380.                     this.rawBufferHeader.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
381.                     this.sysExMessageContent.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
382.                     // Provide start (0xF0) and end (0xF7) of SysEx values
383.                     sysExMessageContent.Text = "F0 F7";
384.                     // Let the user know the expected format of the message
385.                     this.rootPage.NotifyUser("Expecting a string of format 'NN NN NN NN....', where NN is a byte in hex", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
386.                     this.sendButton.IsEnabled = true;
387.                     break;
388. 
389.                 // These messages do not need additional parameters
390.                 case MidiMessageType.ActiveSensing:
391.                 case MidiMessageType.Continue:
392.                 case MidiMessageType.Start:
393.                 case MidiMessageType.Stop:
394.                 case MidiMessageType.SystemReset:
395.                 case MidiMessageType.TimingClock:
396.                 case MidiMessageType.TuneRequest:
397.                     this.sendButton.IsEnabled = true;
398.                     break;
399. 
400.                 default:
401.                     this.sendButton.IsEnabled = false;
402.                     break;
403.             }
404.

406.             // If no further parameters are required, the list is emptied and hidden
407.             UpdateParameterList1();
408.         }

537.                     // This list is for Program Numbers, of which there are 128
538.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter2, 128, "Program Number");
539.                     break;
540. 
541.                 case MidiMessageType.ChannelPressure:
542.                     // This list is for Pressure Values, of which there are 128
543.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter2, 128, "Pressure Value");
544.                     break;
545. 
546.                 case MidiMessageType.PitchBendChange:
547.                     // This list is for Pitch Bend Values, of which there are 16384
548.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter2, 16384, "Pitch Bend Value");
549.                     break;
550. 
551.                 case MidiMessageType.MidiTimeCode:
552.                     // This list is for Values, of which there are 16
553.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter2, 16, "Value");
554.                     break;
555. 
556.                 default:
557.                     // Start with a clean slate
558.                     this.parameter2.Items.Clear();
559. 
560.                     // Hide the first parameter
561.                     this.parameter2.Header = "";
562.                     this.parameter2.IsEnabled = false;
563.                     this.parameter2.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
564.                     break;
565.             }
566.         }
567. 
568.         /// <summary>
569.         /// React to Parameter2 selection change as appropriate
570.         /// </summary>
571.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
572.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
573.         private void Parameter2_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
574.         {
575.             // Find the index of the user's choice
576.             int parameter2SelectedIndex = this.parameter2.SelectedIndex;
577. 
578.             // Some MIDI message types don't need additional parameters past parameter 2
579.             // For them, show the Send button as soon as user selects parameter 2 value from the list
580.             switch (this.currentMessageType)
581.             {
582.                 case MidiMessageType.ProgramChange:
583.                 case MidiMessageType.ChannelPressure:
584.                 case MidiMessageType.PitchBendChange:
585.                 case MidiMessageType.MidiTimeCode:
586. 
587.                     if (parameter2SelectedIndex != -1)
588.                     {
589.                         this.sendButton.IsEnabled = true;
590.                     }
591.                     break;
592. 
593.                 default:
594.                     this.sendButton.IsEnabled = false;
595.                     break;
596.             }
597. 
598.             // Update the third parameter list depending on the second parameter selection
599.             // If no further parameters are required, the list is emptied and hidden
600.             UpdateParameterList3();
601.         }
602. 
603.         /// <summary>
604.         /// For MIDI message types that need the third parameter, populate the list
605.         /// based on the message type. For message types that don't need the third
606.         /// parameter, empty and hide it
607.         /// </summary>
608.         private void UpdateParameterList3()
609.         {
610.             // Do not proceed if Parameter 2 is not chosen
611.             if (this.parameter2.SelectedIndex == -1)
612.             {
613.                 this.parameter3.Items.Clear();
614.                 this.parameter3.Header = "";
615.                 this.parameter3.IsEnabled = false;
616.                 this.parameter3.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
617. 
618.                 return;
619.             }
620. 
621.             switch (this.currentMessageType)
622.             {
623.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOff:
624.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOn:
625.                     // This list is for Velocity Values, of which there are 128
626.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter3, 128, "Velocity");
627.                     break;
628. 
629.                 case MidiMessageType.PolyphonicKeyPressure:
630.                     // This list is for Pressure Values, of which there are 128
631.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter3, 128, "Pressure");
632.                     break;
633. 
634.                 case MidiMessageType.ControlChange:
635.                     // This list is for Values, of which there are 128
636.                     PopulateParameterList(this.parameter3, 128, "Value");
637.                     break;
638. 
639.                 default:
640.                     // Start with a clean slate
641.                     this.parameter3.Items.Clear();
642. 
643.                     // Hide the first parameter
644.                     this.parameter3.Header = "";
645.                     this.parameter3.IsEnabled = false;
646.                     this.parameter3.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
647.                     break;
648.             }
649.         }
650. 
651.         /// <summary>
652.         /// React to Parameter3 selection change as appropriate
653.         /// </summary>
654.         /// <param name="sender">Element that fired the event</param>
655.         /// <param name="e">Event arguments</param>
656.         private void Parameter3_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
657.         {
658.             // Find the index of the user's choice
659.             int parameter3SelectedIndex = this.parameter3.SelectedIndex;
660. 
661.             // The last set of MIDI message types don't need additional parameters
662.             // For them, show the Send button as soon as user selects parameter 3 value from the list
663.             // Set default to disable Send button for any message types that fall through
664.             switch (this.currentMessageType)
665.             {
666.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOff:
667.                 case MidiMessageType.NoteOn:
668.                 case MidiMessageType.PolyphonicKeyPressure:
669.                 case MidiMessageType.ControlChange:
670. 
671.                     if (parameter3SelectedIndex != -1)
672.                     {
673.                         this.sendButton.IsEnabled = true;
674.                     }
675.                     break;
676. 
677.                 default:
678.                     this.sendButton.IsEnabled = false;
679.                     break;
680.             }
681.         }
682. 
683.         /// <summary>
684.         /// Helper function to populate a dropdown lists with options
685.         /// </summary>
686.         /// <param name="list">The parameter list to populate</param>
687.         /// <param name="numberOfOptions">Number of options in the list</param>
688.         /// <param name="listName">The header to display to the user</param>
689.         private void PopulateParameterList(ComboBox list, int numberOfOptions, string listName)
690.         {
691.             // Start with a clean slate
692.             list.Items.Clear();
693. 
694.             // Add the options to the list
695.             for (int i = 0; i < numberOfOptions; i++)
696.             {
697.                 list.Items.Add(i);
698.             }
699. 
700.             // Show the list, so that the user can make the next choice
701.             list.Header = listName;
702.             list.IsEnabled = true;
703.             list.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
704.         }
705.     }

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup Crashes

A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).

**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:

```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```

…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:

```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```

- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.

**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):

| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |

> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.

The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
